```
26	**The big picture:** v1 ships the DQ loop (checks → results → trends → freshness/volume monitors
27	→ alerts → MCP) end-to-end. Post-v1 layers *intelligence* (more expectations, LLM authoring,
28	the remaining monitor kinds), *governance* (admin console, compliance controls), and *scale*
29	(performance hardening, second-impl seams) on top of it.

33	## v1 maturity assessment — where the product honestly stands (2026-07-03)
34	
35	> A calibrated self-assessment recorded at Week-7 close so post-v1 prioritisation starts from
36	> reality, not from the changelog. Verdict: **strong engineering artifact, ~4/10 as a
37	> competitive DQ product today.** Confidence it does what it's configured to do: ~8/10
38	> (live-verified). Confidence a data team should pick it over Soda/Elementary/GX Cloud today:
39	> ~3/10. Confidence the architecture can close the gap: ~7/10 — the seams are the right ones.
40	> One-liner: v1 is a well-engineered, security-conscious **"GX-as-a-service with orchestration
41	> awareness"** — credible as a single-team internal tool; not yet in the conversation with
42	> commercial DQ platforms.
43	
44	**What's genuinely strong (defend without hesitation):**
45	- End-to-end **verified**, not claimed: 4 datasource run paths green against live infra;
46	  orchestration-triggered checks proven (ADF failure → visible in 4m14s; trigger-on-success
47	  firing off real pipeline runs). Trigger-on-pipeline-success is a real differentiator over
48	  cron-only checkers.
49	- The expensive-to-retrofit architecture is already right: monitor-kind seam (ADR 0012),
50	  adapter/runner registries (ADR 0011), `ResultPublisher`, provider-agnostic infra (ADR 0010).
51	- Security/PII posture well above v1 norm: logger-level + column-aware redaction, tested authz
52	  matrix, fail-closed MCP, incident-driven hardening. Alerting has dedup/snooze/severity routing.
53	- MCP integration is ahead of the market curve and tested end-to-end.
54	
55	**The honest gaps (each mapped to where it's tracked):**
56	
57	| # | Gap | Why it matters | Where tracked |
58	|---|---|---|---|
59	| G-a | **Monitors only what you remember to check** — hand-authored expectations; no anomaly baselines, no schema-drift, no auto-suggested checks. Category leaders' core pitch (Monte Carlo/Anomalo) is the inverse: automatic coverage. An unknown-unknown incident sails past DataQ. | Existential for "product"; the single biggest gap | **Theme 1** (remaining monitor kinds: `schema_drift`, `anomaly`) + **Theme 2** (auto-suggestion/LLM authoring) |
60	| G-b | **Scale unproven; structurally weak on 2 of 4 paths** — flat-file + UC runners load the whole file/table into worker pandas; largest table ever validated is a few thousand synthetic rows. No sampling/partition/incremental strategy for checks. (Snowflake pushes down via SQL — fine.) | 100M-row table = worker OOM; blocks any serious deployment | **Theme 7** (perf & scale — add: sampling/partition-aware/incremental check execution as a named workstream) |
61	| G-c | **Every "verified" is self-referential** — one operator, ~1 week live, against a harness built by the same author. Zero real-world data-pathology exposure at scale (schema churn, half-written files, encoding chaos in volume). | Unknown failure modes in the first real deployment | **Theme 10** (test-hardening) + first-real-workload milestone, post-v1 |
62	| G-d | **No incident workflow, no lineage, no ownership routing** — runs + alerts exist; "what broke downstream / who owns it / when was it resolved" doesn't. No data-access audit trail (the HIPAA gate). | This is what DQ products are bought for | **Theme 9** (results/reporting depth) + **Theme 4** / [#431](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/431) (audit trail); lineage/incident objects = new design doc needed; the incident-*narrative* half is design-captured as **Theme 2's agentic root-cause analysis** (2026-07-04); lineage may be *pulled/emitted* rather than built — **Theme 14** governance-catalog + OpenLineage capture (2026-07-04); incident objects anchor to the **asset entity** (Theme 3's Asset-first IA capture, phase 1 = the shared prerequisite) |
63	| G-e | **Single-tenant, config-allowlist admin, one validated IdP** — fine internally, not sellable. | Blocks multi-team/commercial use | **Theme 3** (admin/access) + ADR 0026 / [#461](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/461) (API keys) |
64	| G-f | **Ecosystem: 4 datasources** vs the 30–50 a category product ships; no dbt integration (seam reserved); can't check the Postgres it runs on. | Adoption ceiling | **Theme 8** (datasource depth; generic-RDBMS adapter is the cheap first win) |
65	| G-g | **Engine risk: GX Core pin** — the product's core capability rides a fast-moving third party with documented API drift; DQX swap-in shape exists for UC only. | Strategic dependency | **Theme 2**'s engine-abstraction watch item (below) — no issue until the churn trigger fires |
66	| G-h | **Harness Databricks = Free Edition, non-commercial-only** — the UC demo leg cannot legally back a commercial demo, while ADR 0013's ambition is commercial BYOL. **Decision recorded 2026-07-03 (go-live):** acceptable while the deployment is demo/eval; trigger stands — before any commercial demo/use → paid workspace. | Licence landmine on the demo path | ADR 0021/0013 context — recorded in the go-live checklist ([progress-v1.md](../docs/progress-v1.md)); re-trigger **before any commercial demo** |
67	| G-i | **Pre-marketplace teardown: the deployed app still carries the demo harness** — Flows A/B/C, the 5 harness connections (Snowflake/UC/ADLS/ADF/Airflow), demo suites/users, and the seeded-breach check are all ADR-0021 *test* fixtures, not product. Before any marketplace listing / distributable image / customer-facing deployment (ADR 0013): remove the harness flows + datasource connections + demo users from the reference deployment, and verify nothing in the shipped artifact references harness endpoints. (Noted 2026-07-03 with the G-h decision.) | A demo fixture shipping as if it were product surface — licence (G-h) + credibility + stale-credential risk | ADR 0013 pre-listing checklist item — this row is the note; fold into the marketplace workstream when Theme 2's marketplace work is picked up |
68	
69	**Prioritisation signal for the post-v1 planner:** G-a and G-b are the two that change what the
70	product *is* (coverage-without-authoring + scale-safe execution); G-d is what makes it *usable in
71	anger* (incidents/lineage); the rest are adoption/positioning. Recommended post-v1 opening
72	sequence: Theme 1 `schema_drift` + `anomaly` (rides the ADR 0012 seam, no schema rewrite) →
73	scale-aware execution (G-b, Theme 7) → incident/lineage design doc (G-d).

99	## Theme 2 — DQ-intelligence (expectation expansion, LLM authoring, marketplace)
100	
101	Full design: **[post-v1-dq-intelligence-notes.md](../docs/post-v1-dq-intelligence-notes.md)**. The enabler is
102	already true in v1 — no server-side expectation allowlist, so "add an expectation" is mostly a
103	frontend-catalog + config-validation problem. Four themes: (1) the 5 high-ROI GX built-ins,
104	(2) LLM custom-SQL generator, (3) LLM curated check-suggestions, (4a) curated server-served
105	catalog + allowlist. LLM integration = admin-configured, default-off, BYO-credential `LLMProvider`
106	seam (schema-only, PII-redacted context).
107	
108	| # | Title |
109	|---|---|
110	| [#124](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/124) | Add DQ-dimension classification to checks (Completeness / Uniqueness / Validity / …) |
111	| [#286](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/286) | Apache Iceberg v2 / v3 table-format support |
112	
113	*(The built-ins / LLM / marketplace work is design-captured but not yet filed as discrete issues —
114	file from the detail doc when picked up.)*
115	
116	**Engine-abstraction watch item (from maturity-assessment G-g):** the product's core capability
117	rides a pinned GX Core with documented API drift (CLAUDE.md §11); the DQX swap-in shape exists
118	for the UC runner only. If GX churn continues (or DQX/v1.1 lands), generalise that runner-level
119	engine seam beyond UC so the check engine is a pluggable impl, not a hard dependency. No issue
120	filed yet — file one when the trigger fires.
121	
122	### Agentic root-cause analysis (design-captured 2026-07-04 — the category-leading bet)
123	
124	**When a check fails, DataQ investigates — it doesn't just alert.** Every DQ product today stops
125	at detection ("row count dropped 40% 🔴") and a human spends the next two hours on why. DataQ's
126	moat for closing that loop already exists in the v1 schema: the `triggered_by`
127	`pipeline_runs` ↔ `runs` correlation (cron-only checkers structurally lack it), `metric_value` as
128	a SQL-aggregatable trend scalar (ADR 0012), the column profiler, and redacted failing samples
129	(#417). Two-layer, LLM-degradable design:
130	
131	1. **Deterministic evidence card (no LLM anywhere).** On a `fail`/`critical` result, a Celery
132	   task assembles the dossier from existing data: the upstream pipeline run (status + duration/
133	   delay vs. its own history), the check's `metric_value` trend ("sudden vs. slow drift"), a
134	   profile-diff of the failing batch vs. the last passing one (which segment broke), sibling
135	   checks on the same table, and downstream suites (blast radius). Ships as a structured card on
136	   the alert via the existing `ResultPublisher` seam. Most of the triage value lives here, with
137	   zero new dependencies — **build this first**.
138	2. **LLM narrative + ranked causal hypothesis (optional).** Turns the dossier into a
139	   three-sentence diagnosis. Rides the **same admin-configured, default-off, BYO-credential
140	   `LLMProvider` seam** as the authoring work above (one seam, two features; endpoint/key in the
141	   SecretStore like any connection; any OpenAI-compatible/Anthropic/Azure-OpenAI/local endpoint).
142	   Context is schema-only + column-aware-redacted samples — the model sees what a non-admin user
143	   would. **Fail-open:** no LLM connection configured → layer 1 still ships in full.
144	
145	**Zero-config interactive path:** the MCP server (Theme 13) exposes the same dossier as tools, so
146	a user's *own* Claude/Copilot is the reasoning engine and DataQ never holds an LLM key — the
147	push-narrative-into-the-alert version is the premium ergonomics, not a gate.
148	
149	This is the **incident-narrative half of gap G-d** done LLM-natively, and it compounds with
150	Theme 1 (anomaly baselines sharpen the dossier) and Theme 5 (the card enriches the alert
151	payload). No issues filed yet — file the two layers as separate issues when picked up (layer 1
152	has no LLM dependency and can ship alone).
153

156	## Theme 3 — Admin, access model & UI/IA
157	
158	Full design: **[post-v1-admin-ui-notes.md](../docs/post-v1-admin-ui-notes.md)**. v1 ships suite-level sharing
159	+ a read-only workspace-admin view (#289). A full RBAC console is gold-plating for a single-tenant
160	trusted-team tool; the market leaders lead with checks→results→trends→alerts, not user-lifecycle.
161	
162	| # | Title |
163	|---|---|
164	| [#411](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/411) | Workspace-admin: workspace-wide view on Dashboard + Results (both are owned-or-shared scoped today) |
165	| [#412](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/412) | Admin page is read-only — allow workspace-admin write actions (manage shares / suites) |
166	| [#461](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/461) | **DataQ-issued API keys / service tokens** (REST + MCP) — see below |
167	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Data-asset-centric view** — the biggest IA gap (added 2026-07-04): the UI is suite-centric but users think table-centric ("is `orders` healthy?"). A dataset page aggregating every check, result, freshness state, and trend across all suites targeting that table/file pattern. The natural UI anchor for Theme 14's lineage/governance pull and the Theme-2 RCA blast radius; category leaders are asset-first for this reason. **Phase 2 of the Asset-first IA capture below** — build the asset entity first. |
168	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Global search / command palette (⌘K)** — jump to any suite/check/connection/run by name. The list APIs exist; disproportionate daily-use payoff for a small build. |
169	| _(no issue yet)_ | **First-run onboarding + empty-state pass** — guided connect → suite → check → run path and designed empty states. Low value for the current solo deployment, first-touch-critical for any marketplace/BYOL evaluator (ADR 0013); cheap to retrofit now vs. embarrassing at listing time. |
170	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Bulk operations on checks** — multi-select enable/disable/severity/snooze; one-at-a-time editing doesn't survive 50-check suites. |
171	
172	**DataQ-issued API keys / service tokens (#461, ADR [0026](../docs/adr/0026-auth-api-keys-and-principal-seam.md) proposed).** Auth today is Azure-AD-only (delegated/SSO) for both REST and `/mcp` — the deepest remaining vendor lock-in (the `get_current_user` seam has one real impl; `users.aad_object_id` is Azure-shaped) and it blocks BYOL-on-AWS/GCP (ADR 0013) and headless/programmatic access (a long-lived scoped key beats a ~60-min refreshing token for CI / always-on MCP clients). The fix is a **second authenticator behind the same `get_current_user` seam** so the **REST API and MCP accept it identically** — never MCP-only — which also finally *exercises* the seam (ADR 0010). Phase it: **user-scoped PATs first** (inherit the owner's per-suite grants → zero new authz; optional read-only down-scope), defer standalone **service-account principals** (they force generalizing `aad_object_id` → a generic principal with pluggable identity bindings + non-user suite sharing). Credential bar: hashed-at-rest + show-once + prefix + expiry + revocation + audit, in a new `api_keys` table (not the retrievable-secret SecretStore), with key lifecycle tied to the owner so it can't outlive a deactivated account.
173	
174	### Accessibility & inclusive UI (design-captured 2026-07-04 — a genuine blind spot)
175	
176	Nothing in the repo mentions accessibility today — flagged in the 2026-07-04 UI review as the
177	gap most likely to convert from "nice" to **procurement checkbox** the moment a commercial buyer
178	with compliance requirements appears (it belongs next to Theme 4 in the "sellability" bucket).
179	Target: **WCAG 2.1 AA**, phased so it's cheap early instead of a retrofit crisis:
180	
181	1. **Automated floor first** — wire `axe-core` into the existing Playwright E2E lane (25 specs
182	   already run in CI) + `vitest-axe` for component tests; fail CI on new serious/critical
183	   violations. This is the ratchet that stops regression before any manual work starts.
184	2. **Non-color severity cues** — severity is communicated almost entirely by color today
185	   (red/orange/green tags, chart series). Add icons/shapes/text alongside color and adopt a
186	   colorblind-safe palette (also fixes the recharts dashboards, where color is the *only*
187	   encoding). Highest user-impact single item — ~8% of male users can't reliably read the
188	   current severity language.
189	3. **Keyboard & focus audit** — full keyboard traversal of the 13-screen set; focus-trap +
190	   restore on the surviving drawers/modals (Share, version history, run progress, import);
191	   visible focus states; skip-to-content. antd gives a decent baseline; the custom
192	   drawer/table/editor compositions are where it breaks.
193	4. **Screen-reader semantics** — landmarks, table headers/captions on the results/runs tables,
194	   aria-live for async run-progress updates, and **text alternatives for every chart**
195	   (the dashboards are pure-visual today; a data-table fallback per chart covers both a11y and
196	   the PDF-export path, #345).
197	5. **Related, decide-later:** reduced-motion support (cheap, ride item 2), dark mode (already
198	   Theme 12), i18n/locale externalization (defer until a non-English prospect exists — but stop
199	   hard-coding user-facing strings in new code *now*, which costs nothing).
200	
201	No issues filed yet — file items 1–4 as separate issues when picked up; item 1 can land
202	independently as a Theme-10-style CI ratchet.
203	
204	### Asset-first IA (design-captured 2026-07-04 — the lineage-era navigation model)
205	
206	**When lineage lands (Theme 14), invert the navigation from suite-first to asset-first — but
207	split the noun from the verb rather than replacing one with the other.** Assets become what
208	users *browse and reason about* (health, lineage, incidents, blast radius); suites remain how
209	checks *execute* (batching, scheduling, trigger bindings, notifications, sharing). Making assets
210	primary for *everything* would fight three structural facts: authz is suite-scoped (ADR 0027)
211	and re-keying permissions to assets is a painful migration for little gain; scheduling /
212	triggers / notifications are genuinely batch-level operational concepts; and GX itself is
213	suite-shaped, so the engine grain stays suites regardless. The products that do this well (dbt
214	models-vs-jobs, Monte Carlo assets-vs-monitors) all run this two-axis model.
215	
216	**The enabling primitive — build once, four features consume it:** a first-class **asset
217	entity**. Today "the table" exists only implicitly inside `Suite.target` and check configs.
218	Lineage needs asset *nodes*; the asset page needs asset *identity*; G-d incidents need something
219	to *attach to*; governance-catalog sync (Theme 14) needs an entity to *map to*. One migration
220	serves all four — schedule it before any of them.
221	
222	**Asset identity = the OpenLineage dataset naming spec (namespace + name), adopted as the
223	canonical key from day one.** It answers the hardest resolution problems (the same logical table
224	via DEV vs QA connections, flat-file patterns as assets, UC three-level names) with a
225	vendor-neutral convention, and makes the Theme-14 OpenLineage emission/pull interop automatic
226	instead of a mapping layer.
227	
228	**Phases (each shippable alone):**
229	1. **Asset entity + resolution** — `assets` table keyed by OpenLineage naming + the mapping of
230	   checks/runs to asset IDs. The migration everything else rides.
231	2. **Asset view** (the row above) as a read-only aggregation. Authz subtlety: an asset page
232	   aggregates *across* suites, so it must filter to the caller's suite grants and never leak
233	   the existence of unshared checks — the same 404-no-leak discipline verified live at go-live.
234	3. **Lineage attach** — asset pages grow upstream/downstream + blast radius; the Theme-2 RCA
235	   dossier and G-d incident objects anchor to assets.
236	4. **Navigation inversion** — sidebar leads with Assets, dashboard health aggregates per asset
237	   (more meaningful to data consumers than per-suite), suites demote to an "execution groups"
238	   surface; suite CRUD and deep links keep working untouched. This is the step where DataQ
239	   stops feeling like "GX-as-a-service" and starts feeling like a data-observability product —
240	   the G-a/G-d repositioning the maturity assessment calls for.
241	
242	No issues filed yet — phase 1 files first when picked up (it's the dependency of the other
243	three and of Theme 14's governance sync).
244

247	## Theme 4 — Compliance (GDPR / CCPA-CPRA / HIPAA)
248	
249	Full design: **[compliance-posture.md](../docs/compliance-posture.md)**. v1 is privacy-by-design (logger PII
250	redaction, default-redact samples, retention purge, SecretStore, suite-scoped authz, BYOL
251	controller/processor split). These close the gaps for a credible v2.x "processor-grade controls" claim.
252	
253	| # | Gap | Regime hook |
254	|---|---|---|
255	| [#431](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/431) | 🔴 **G1** data-access audit trail (who read which result/sample) | HIPAA §164.312(b) — the HIPAA gate |
256	| [#432](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/432) | 🟠 **G2** data-subject-rights machinery (erasure / access / portability) | GDPR Art 15/17/20, CCPA |
257	| [#433](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/433) | 🟠 **G3** authoritative PII/PHI classification (warehouse tags over name heuristic) | — |
258	| [#434](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/434) | 🟠 **G4** region/residency assertion + enforcement | GDPR Ch. V; LLM transfer vector |
259	| [#435](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/435) | 🟡 **G5** assert encryption-at-rest in IaC + offer CMK | — |
260	
261	### Privacy pack (design-captured 2026-07-04)
262	
263	Builds on **G3/#433** (authoritative warehouse-tag classification) and **absorbs the
264	classification remainder** left open when #415 closed with #417's column-aware redaction —
265	the new pieces, no issues filed yet:
266	
267	- **Profiler-side PII auto-detection** as an additional classification *source*: the profiler
268	  already visits every column; lightweight format/regex detectors (no LLM) persist a per-column
269	  classification tag, and redaction reads **tags** instead of name heuristics. Classification
270	  sources compose: warehouse tags (#433) > governance-catalog pull (Theme 14) > profiler
271	  heuristics, most-authoritative wins.
272	- **`pii_drift` monitor kind** — "a column that looks like email addresses just appeared in a
273	  table not classified as containing PII." Rides the ADR 0012 seam next to Theme 1's
274	  `schema_drift`; the alert privacy teams actually fear, and one mainstream DQ tools don't fire.
275	- **Zero-sample "privacy mode"** — a deployment-level switch where failing-row samples are
276	  never persisted (aggregates + `metric_value` + unexpected-counts only). Mostly a write-path
277	  gate; turns the existing redaction stack into a tiered posture: full → column-aware
278	  redacted (#417) → zero-sample. The first question HIPAA-tier / EU deployments ask.
279

283	
284	v1 ships the `ResultPublisher` seam with Teams + Slack + email, severity routing, dedup, snooze.
285	These enrich and de-risk it:
286	
287	| # | Title |
288	|---|---|
289	| [#416](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/416) | Enrich Slack/email alerts: deep link to run, per-check expected-vs-observed, actionable sample, run metadata |
290	| [#415](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/415) | Actionable failing-row samples: column-aware redaction (PII vs identifier vs safe) — *closed with #417; the classification remainder is design-captured in Theme 4's privacy pack (2026-07-04)* |
291	| [#386](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/386) | Tie `dedup._RANK` to a shared severity source so it can't drift from `routing.route_for` |
292	| [#387](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/387) | `suppression.py` should early-return `False` on `run.status == 'failed'` |
293	| [#388](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/388) | Single-source the `alert_on` literals (model CHECK ↔ validation) to prevent drift |
294	| [#389](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/389) | Rename `teams_webhook_secret_name` → channel-neutral before a 2nd ResultPublisher ships |
295	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Generic HMAC-signed outbound-webhook `ResultPublisher`** — one publisher makes the alerting side vendor-neutral: signed JSON POST (mirroring the Airflow *ingest* signing pattern), payloads pass the same redaction rules. Prerequisite: the #389 channel-neutral rename. |
296	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Per-destination payload templates + auth header** on the webhook publisher — a thin template layer gives **create-only coverage of PagerDuty (Events API v2), Opsgenie, ServiceNow (inbound REST), and Jira (Automation inbound webhooks) with zero vendor-specific code** in DataQ. Bidirectional/ITSM-grade sync is Theme 14, deliberately separate. |
297	| [#492](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/492) | **ADF webhook live delivery (deferred from v1).** Azure Log Alerts V2 drop query rows (only dimensions; `runId` high-cardinality); ADF metric alerts are aggregate (no `runId`); v1 alerting is per-suite with **no workspace/orchestration-failure channel**. Revisit needs either a workspace alert channel + metric-alert→bound-suite attribution (failure-alert), or Log-Analytics diagnostics + a dimension-split scheduled-query rule / Logic-App reshaper (per-run). The receiver + in-app URL generator already shipped; the live all-status poll covers ADF monitoring for v1. See the #492 deferral note for full caveats |

327	## Theme 8 — Datasource / connection depth
328	
329	| # | Title |
330	|---|---|
331	| [#194](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/194) | Snowflake key-pair: support encrypted (passphrase-protected) private keys |
332	| [#195](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/195) | Snowflake key-pair: migrate GX runner off deprecated `kwargs['connect_args']` private_key path |
333	| [#351](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/351) | Test Connection button on the New/Edit connection page (draft-connection test endpoint) |
334	| [#244](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/244) | Suite-on-suite triggering (run a suite when another suite completes) |
335	| [#466](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/466) | Interactive datasource browsing — container browser (ADLS/S3) + 3-level catalog→schema→table picker (UC); from the W1–6 not-started triage (run/check paths shipped via explicit targets; browsing superseded & deferred) |
336	| _(no issue yet)_ | JSON flat-file support — `FlatFileCheckRunner`/profiler/run-target accept `json` alongside `csv`/`parquet` (the W2 file-asset config task listed JSON; v1 shipped CSV/Parquet only) |
337	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Generic PostgreSQL adapter** — `ConnectionAdapter` + thin `CheckRunner` on the shared `gx_runner` (same shape as the Snowflake path; GX supports Postgres natively via SQLAlchemy). One **engine-generic** adapter (never an Azure-branded one — ADR 0010/0013) covers Azure Database for PostgreSQL, **Azure HorizonDB** (fully PG-compatible — standard connection strings/drivers; *Preview* as of 2026-07, so support it as "it's Postgres" and don't advertise a named integration until GA), and AWS RDS / GCP Cloud SQL / self-hosted for free. Dogfoodable against the app's own Postgres, so integration tests need no new harness infra. This is the G-f "generic-RDBMS cheap first win". |
338	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Generic MSSQL / T-SQL adapter** — one adapter covers **Microsoft Fabric** (Warehouse + Lakehouse SQL analytics endpoint + Fabric SQL database — all standard SQL Server TDS endpoints, port 1433, ODBC 18+ / pyodbc; GX supports mssql via SQLAlchemy), **Azure SQL Database**, Synapse dedicated pools — and, engine-generic like the PG row (ADR 0010/0013), any standard SQL Server endpoint (on-prem, AWS RDS for SQL Server). The one real design cost: Fabric endpoints want **Entra ID auth** (user or service principal; SQL auth unsupported on some Fabric items) → the adapter needs a client-credentials token flow — same KV-held secret model already used for ADF. Bonus: anything mirrored into Fabric (Cosmos DB, HorizonDB, …) becomes checkable through its mirror's SQL analytics endpoint with zero extra adapter code. |
339	| _(no issue yet)_ | **OneLake flat-file spike** — OneLake speaks the ADLS Gen2 DFS API (`onelake.dfs.fabric.microsoft.com`); verify the existing ADLS flat-file adapter reaches Fabric lake files with just an endpoint override + Entra auth before promising it. Spike first, then a small extension — not a new adapter. |
340	| _(no issue yet)_ | **S3-compatible `endpoint_url` override** on the existing S3 adapter — one config field unlocks MinIO, Cloudflare R2, and on-prem object stores (ADR 0010/0013 vendor-neutral; same shape as the OneLake spike). |
341	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Google BigQuery adapter** — the single biggest warehouse omission: Snowflake/Databricks/BigQuery is the modern top-three and DataQ covers two. GX supports it via `sqlalchemy-bigquery`; same shape as the Snowflake path (dialect + connection-spec + service-account-key auth in the SecretStore). |
342	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Amazon Redshift adapter** — the AWS warehouse; pairs with the existing S3 adapter (AWS shops almost always run both). `sqlalchemy-redshift` is Postgres-dialect-adjacent → a near-sibling of the generic PG adapter. |
343	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Google Cloud Storage flat-file adapter** — completes the object-store trio next to ADLS Gen2 + S3 (`gcsfs`; the `FlatFileCheckRunner` + batch resolution already do the hard part). With BigQuery + Redshift this yields the ADR 0013 BYOL claim: **warehouse + object store covered on all three clouds**. |
344	| _(no issue yet)_ | **MySQL / MariaDB adapter** — the other half of the open-source OLTP world; a dialect swap on the engine-generic SQLAlchemy runner once the PG adapter proves the pattern. Enormous installed base, near-zero marginal cost. |
345	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Trino adapter (+ Amazon Athena)** — the G-f multiplier: one SQLAlchemy dialect inherits the user's entire Trino/Starburst connector ecosystem (Hive, Iceberg, Cassandra, Mongo, Kafka-topics-as-tables — anything their cluster federates) with no per-store adapters on our side; **Athena** rides the same dialect family for serverless AWS. The cheapest answer to "4 datasources vs the 30–50 a category product ships". |
346	
347	**Recommended order** (decided 2026-07-03, extended 2026-07-04; sits alongside — not competing
348	with — the Theme-1 opening sequence; different layer of the stack): PG adapter → MSSQL/Fabric
349	adapter → OneLake spike; then the cloud-triad completion (BigQuery → Redshift → GCS → MySQL),
350	with Trino/Athena slotted whenever federation demand shows up (it can jump the queue — it's the
351	G-f multiplier).
352	
353	**Decided-against (2026-07-03): no native Cosmos DB adapter.** Cosmos has no SQL dialect
354	SQLAlchemy/GX can drive, and the DataFrame-reader alternative (Cosmos SDK → pandas, the UC-runner
355	shape) has RU-cost + scale problems that make it a bad default. Coverage path of record: **via its
356	Fabric mirror** → the MSSQL adapter reads the mirror's SQL analytics endpoint. Revisit a native
357	adapter only on real demand.
358	
359	**Demand-driven boundary (recorded 2026-07-04) — major but deliberately not default:**
360	Oracle / Teradata / SAP HANA (real enterprise demand, heavy drivers + licensing — build on a
361	concrete prospect, not speculatively); **MongoDB** (same no-SQL-dialect logic as Cosmos, same
362	routing answer: via Trino's connector or a mirror); **Kafka / streaming** (not batch — that's the
363	DQX v1.1 lane, ADR [0003](../docs/adr/0003-gx-only-for-v1.md)); **Delta / Iceberg table formats
364	on raw object storage** — Iceberg is already tracked as [#286](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/286)
365	(Theme 2); a `delta` flat-file format would slot next to the JSON row above.
366

432	## Theme 13 — MCP tool expansion (candidate endpoints)
433	
434	The v1 MCP server exposes 8 curated tools (ADR [0008](../docs/adr/0008-mcp-server.md));
435	the REST surface has ~52 more endpoints. Candidates below, tiered by risk — every new
436	tool stays a thin service-layer wrapper with `require_permission` authz + sample
437	redaction, exactly like the existing 8 (`backend/app/mcp/server.py`). Cross-cutting
438	dependencies: **#488** (workspace-admin visibility in MCP tools) and **#461 / ADR 0026**
439	(DataQ-issued API keys, which unblock headless MCP clients).
440	
441	**Filed (2026-07-04, carried from the retired `WEEK8_TODO` working tracker):**
442	
443	| # | Title |
444	|---|---|
445	| [#583](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/583) | `profile_column` 422s on SQL suites without explicit `table`/`schema` — default to the suite's run target (found in the #550 MCP client E2E) |
446	| [#584](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/584) | NL tool-selection spot-check — watch a real LLM client route the 4 canonical queries (descriptions are LLM-facing, CLAUDE.md §10; the selection in #550 was author-made) |
447	
448	**Tier 1 — high-value safe reads:**
449	
450	| Candidate tool | Wraps | NL query it serves |
451	|---|---|---|
452	| `list_checks` / `get_check` | `GET /suites/{id}/checks[/{id}]` | "what checks does the orders suite have?" |
453	| `get_check_history` | `GET .../checks/{id}/history` | "how has null-rate trended this week?" |
454	| `list_runs` | `GET /runs` (filterable) | "show me yesterday's failed runs" |
455	| `get_run_results` | `GET /runs/{id}` (redacted) | "why did run X fail?" |
456	| `list_connections` | `GET /connections` — names/types/health **only, never config or secrets** | "which connections are unhealthy?" |
457	| `list_schedules` | `GET /schedules` | "when does the orders suite run?" |
458	| `list_trigger_bindings` | `GET /trigger-bindings` | "what triggers the gold suite?" |
459	| `get_notification_config` | `GET /suites/{id}/notifications` | "who gets alerted for this suite?" |
460	| `get_suite_performance` | `GET /dashboard/summary` (per-suite slice) | "which suite is worst this month?" |
461	| `export_suite` | `GET /suites/{id}/export` | "export the orders suite" |
462	
463	**Tier 2 — mutating, edit-permission-gated:**
464	
465	| Candidate tool | Wraps | Note |
466	|---|---|---|
467	| `dryrun_check` | `POST /suites/{id}/checks/dryrun` | the LLM author-preview loop — pairs with `create_check` |
468	| `update_check` / `delete_check` | `PATCH`/`DELETE` check | delete needs confirm-style ergonomics |
469	| `snooze_check` / `unsnooze_check` | check snooze endpoints | "snooze this alert for 24h" |
470	| `cancel_run` | `POST /runs/{id}/cancel` | |
471	| `create_schedule` / `delete_schedule` | schedules CRUD | "run this suite daily at 9am IST" |
472	| `create_trigger_binding` | `POST /trigger-bindings` | "run this suite when the orders DAG succeeds" |
473	| `import_suite` | `POST /suites/import` | |
474	| `suggest_column_policy` | `POST /suites/{id}/column-policy/suggest` | redaction-policy assistant |
475	| `test_connection` | `POST /connections/{id}/test` | action but non-destructive |

477	**Excluded (deliberate):** connection create/update/reauth (**credentials transiting an
478	LLM — hard no**); the orchestration webhooks (M2M surface); admin endpoints (workspace-
479	admin scoping pending #488); share mutations (permission escalation through a
480	conversational interface); `_probe` (demo-only); `/me` + `/users/search` (low value).

482	---
483	
484	## Theme 14 — Ecosystem & vendor-neutral portability (added 2026-07-04)
485	
486	Meet users in the tools they already run — incident/ITSM, test management, data governance,
487	GitOps — and keep every integration behind a seam with **open standards first** (ADR 0010/0013:
488	no tool becomes architecture; each vendor is one impl). All design-captured, no issues filed yet.
489	
490	### Checks-as-code: `dataq.yaml` + a CI gate
491	
492	Formalize the existing export/import format into a **versioned declarative suite format** plus a
493	CLI / GitHub Action: `dataq validate` in a data-pipeline repo's CI, `dataq apply` to sync,
494	**drift detection** between repo and workspace. The market leaders (Soda, GX Cloud) lead with
495	this workflow; DataQ's angle is that the format **round-trips with a full UI and the
496	suite-scoped authz model** — checks-as-code tools mostly have no real UI, UI tools no real
497	GitOps. Hard dependency: **ADR 0026 / #461** (PATs) — this is its killer use case, and the
498	argument for scheduling PATs early in the cycle.
499	
500	### Incident / ITSM — tier 2, bidirectional
501	
502	Tier 1 (create-only PagerDuty / Opsgenie / ServiceNow / Jira via the generic signed-webhook
503	publisher + payload templates) lives in **Theme 5** and needs zero vendor code. Tier 2 is the
504	ITSM-grade half: DataQ incident objects (the G-d design) sync *both ways* — ack/resolve in
505	PagerDuty/ServiceNow reflects in DataQ, Jira issue links attach to results — and the **Theme 2
506	agentic-RCA evidence card is the incident payload**, so the ticket arrives with the diagnosis
507	pre-attached. Per-vendor impls behind one `IncidentProvider`-shaped seam, only after tier 1
508	proves demand.
509	
510	### Test-management publishing (TestRail, Xray, Zephyr)
511	
512	DQ artifacts map cleanly: suites → test plans, checks → cases, runs → test runs with results.
513	A result-*exporter* seam (a `ResultPublisher` sibling — per-run push, not per-alert) publishes
514	run outcomes into the test-management tool of record; pairs with Theme 9's PDF export (#345)
515	as the "reporting into someone else's system of record" family.
516	
517	### Data-governance catalogs (+ OpenLineage)
518	
519	Two directions, one seam:
520	
521	- **Pull** — consume the catalog's glossary/PII classifications as an authoritative
522	  classification source (feeds Theme 4's privacy pack + #433, ranked above profiler
523	  heuristics), and **pull lineage** to power the RCA blast-radius (Theme 2) and gap G-d
524	  instead of building a lineage graph ourselves.
525	- **Push** — publish DQ results as **quality facets/assertions on catalog entities**, so data
526	  consumers see check status where they shop for data. DataHub and OpenMetadata both have
527	  first-class assertion/data-quality APIs — natural open-source reference impls; Microsoft
528	  Purview covers the Azure story; Collibra / Atlan / Alation are commercial targets behind the
529	  same seam.
530	- **Open standard first: emit OpenLineage events** from runs — one vendor-neutral event stream
531	  consumed by Marquez, DataHub, Purview, Atlan and the orchestrators' own lineage backends;
532	  cheaper and more neutral than any point integration, and the likely first slice.
533	
534	**Shared primitive:** this whole section keys on the **asset entity** (Theme 3's Asset-first IA
535	capture, phase 1) with **OpenLineage dataset naming as the canonical identity** — build that
536	first; catalog mapping, lineage nodes, and quality-facet publishing all resolve through it.
537	
538	### Observability & deploy portability
539	
540	Completes the last half-open ADR 0010 seam (tracked issues previously unmapped in this doc):
541	
542	| # | Title |
543	|---|---|
544	| [#524](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/524) | Migrate the log pipeline opencensus → OTel (opencensus is EOL; spans already OTel-native via #525) |
545	| _(no issue yet)_ | **Generic OTLP exporter endpoint** (`OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT`) — App Insights becomes one backend among any OTLP consumer (Grafana/Tempo, Datadog, Jaeger); the observability twin of the `DATAQ_AUTH_*` generic-OIDC cutover (ADR 0028), and a BYOL/marketplace prerequisite in spirit (ADR 0013) |
546	| [#505](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/505) | AWS + GCP deploy IaC behind the provider-agnostic seams (ADR 0028 follow-up) |
547
```

```
286	**Current week:** **v1 DONE — `v1.0.0` tagged 2026-07-04 (Week 8 closed 29/29 + exit gate MET; epic #177 + the W8 milestone closed; retro at [docs/retro-v1.md](docs/retro-v1.md); next: post-v1 cycle planning from [context/post-v1-roadmap.md](context/post-v1-roadmap.md)).** The gates are live in CI: backend `--cov-fail-under=80` in `pyproject.toml` (98.3% / 1273 tests on main, after closing the four sub-80% modules — #557) + frontend `lines: 80` over ALL of `src/` via `pnpm test:coverage` (87.8% / 334 tests — #558). The W8 batch #556–#560 also closed #385 (CORS activation tests, #556), #205 (catalog↔GX contract test, #559), #352 (dashboard Avg-Duration + real deltas, #560), and #128 (full-stack E2E — the gates were its last half). **Go-live close (2026-07-03/04, all cleared):** pre-tag QA — qa-verifier workout NO-GO on the NUL-byte 500 (#567) → fixed #570 (also closed #371) → re-run GO (21 injection points 422, zero 500s); prod redeployed `2fa05333` + re-probed live; live prod workout as non-admin Olivia 15/15 + webhook-auth hostility 7/7 401s (#569 closed, both halves); **ops/renewals timers consciously SKIPPED** (demo-scoped credentials; expiry self-signals via #419 alerting; recovery = re-mint + KV update; G-i teardown covers the end state). Checklist progress 2026-07-03: #553 closed (#562, bare pip-audit green) · mutation spike done (mutmut `dashboard_service` 436/436 killed; Stryker 82.35%; survivors → #563, config retarget #564) · prod deploy + smoke re-green done (`8dee4f4a` images; Flows A/B/C `succeeded` as dataq-admin — flat-file suite recreated post-#540; Azure CLI pre-authorized on the API scope for non-interactive bearers, #565 + TF import) · decisions recorded: **ADR 0026 deferred post-v1** (PATs-first shape confirmed; Basic auth rejected — see the ADR's decision record) + **Databricks Free-Edition** (demo/eval OK, paid workspace before commercial use — gap G-h) + **pre-marketplace harness teardown** (gap G-i: strip Flows A/B/C + harness connections + demo users before any marketplace/customer-facing artifact; also deploy/README.md). **Week 7 — Deployment, hardening & docs — COMPLETE (41/41, closed 2026-07-03; milestone + epic #176 closed); DataQ v1 is DEPLOYED TO AZURE and reachable** (Weeks 1–7 complete, all exit gates met). **Cloud deploy (2026-06-28):** the in-repo Terraform (`deploy/terraform/azure/`, ADR 0024) stood up the app stack into `dataq-rg` — `dataq-app-{api,worker}` + `dataq-app-migrate` job (GHCR slim image, ADR 0025) on the **shared `dataq-cae`** Container Apps env, `dataq-app-redis` (password-auth), Key Vault (UAMI) + App Insights + Log Analytics, and **`dataq-app-web`** Static Web App with the api **linked as same-origin `/api` backend**. The app's DB is a distinct **`dataq`** database + least-priv **`dataq_app`** role on the **shared `dataq-pg-wus3-*`** server (1-of-each free/trial cap → env + Postgres shared with the harness, neutral-named `purpose=dataq-shared`; harness Postgres backed-up→recreated→restored). Azure AD **SSO app registrations** (API + SPA) created in TF + wired; migrate job ran `alembic upgrade head`; API healthy (401 = auth-enforced), SPA + deep-links 200, GitHub OIDC secrets/vars + `production` env set. Fixed **#393** (opencensus AzureLogHandler `lock=None` on Py3.13) en route. **GHCR package→repo connect done** (Actions-access grant → CI's `GITHUB_TOKEN` can push) and the **Deploy workflow validated end-to-end** (#403 fixed the migrate-command + frontend-pnpm bugs #401/#402; build→push→`alembic upgrade head`→ACA roll + SWA deploy all green on `v6`). **Post-deploy hardening (2026-06-28):** two production bugs surfaced and fixed — **#405** (Celery beat crashed on startup: the embedded `worker -B` beat re-nulled `self.lock` inside the opencensus `AzureLogHandler.createLock` fork on Python 3.13, silently killing ALL periodic tasks — orchestration polling, scheduled-suite dispatch, gap recovery, and sample-failure purge; fixed by making `createLock` idempotent in `backend/app/core/logging.py` + a network-free regression test; **#407** merged) and **#406** (deployed app couldn't read Key Vault: `AzureKeyVaultStore` called `DefaultAzureCredential()` with no args but the api+worker container runs a USER-assigned managed identity and `AZURE_CLIENT_ID` was unset — blocked connection tests, suite runs, AND orchestration polling; fixed by adding `AZURE_CLIENT_ID = azurerm_user_assigned_identity.app.client_id` to `local.app_env` in `deploy/terraform/azure/containerapps.tf`; **#408** merged). Backend image `:v7` built+pushed from main (with #405+#406); api+worker rolled to v7; App Insights re-enabled on the worker (the #405 mitigation of temporarily dropping `APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING` on the worker is reverted) — landed as **#409** (`image_tag` default v4→v7). **Orchestration polling is now live end-to-end** — beat starts clean (zero NoneType crashes) with App Insights on, api healthy (401, auth-enforced), ADF+Airflow connections polling via the 10-min beat fallback, Key Vault secrets read successfully. **Post-deploy feature batch (2026-06-29, all merged; prod image `:v7`→`:v10`):** Slack + email alert publishers behind the `ResultPublisher` seam (#413, `:v8`), column-aware failing-sample redaction (#417) + the #383/#384/#395/#423 hardening batch (`:v9`, bump #414), URL-encode DB password (#421/#395), always-alert operationally-failed runs (#419), alerting upsert race fix (#420/#384), per-run check outcome in the runs table (#425/#423), mypy gate over `backend/tests` (#418), and — **pulling ADR 0012 post-v1 Theme A forward — freshness & volume monitor-kinds end-to-end** (run engine #426 + authoring path & check-editor UI #437, `:v10`, bump #438). Three post-v1 design docs landed (#422/#430/#436) and are consolidated in **[context/post-v1-roadmap.md](context/post-v1-roadmap.md)** (the single post-v1 home + week-wise-task-generator input). **W7 in-repo work now DONE:** the **FastMCP 8-tool server** at `/mcp` (Azure-AD `JWTVerifier`-validated, fail-closed; ADR 0008, #460); the **hardening/docs pass** — prod-docs gate (#464), Swagger completeness + error-shape audit (#465), deployment guide + complete env-var reference (#468); **consistency hardening** — trigger-dedup index (#456, closes #308) + stuck-run reaper (#458, closes #309); the **visual-fidelity pass** (#459); and the W1–6 deferred + not-started triages (#463/#467, closing #169/#170). **W7 close-out batch (2026-07-01/02, all merged):** OTel **request/task spans** to App Insights (#525 — vendor-neutral core `backend/app/core/tracing.py` + Azure exporter-only, module-scope FastAPI + producer/consumer Celery instrumentation, secret/PII-safe span attributes, `dataq.request_id` span↔log join; opencensus→OTel log migration → #524) · **vault lazy-import test coverage** (#523, `secrets.py` 100%) · **#17 MCP polish** (#522 — this file's `.mcp.json` appendix + CONTRIBUTING rule 39 (numbered 38 pre-#547)) · **Playwright E2E expansion** (#526 schedules/triggers/notifications panels + #527 run-detail sample/dashboard/check-editor variants/admin — 25 specs green in CI) · **opt-in live-smoke lane** (#531 — `frontend/e2e-live/` gated on `E2E_LIVE_BASE_URL` with captured-OIDC session + `e2e_smoke.py` `DATAQ_BEARER` mode + runbook checklist; never in CI) · **user-docs enrichment** (#528 — notifications/scheduling/best-practices/feature-matrix pages; #532 filed) · **MCP tool-expansion candidates** (#530 — post-v1 Theme 13 + issue #529). **LIVE SMOKE RUN + #492 DONE (2026-07-02, via the #531 lane):** browser lane 3/3 · `e2e_smoke.py` bearer-mode vs prod · **Flows A/B/C verified green** (Snowflake ×3 / UC / flat-file) · **#525 spans confirmed in App Insights** · **MCP 4-query protocol smoke passed** vs live `/mcp` · **#492 closed** — Action Group + `PipelineFailedRuns` metric alert on the harness factory; a deliberate `pl_dataq_smoke_fail` failure was visible in DataQ **4m14s** after fire (Common-Alert-Schema → `AlertPing` → immediate-poll ingest, #534). Smoke fallout fixed same-day and deployed: UC dialect regression (#535→#537), traceback-locals credential leak (#536→#538 — **Databricks PAT rotation required**), suite-delete FK cascade (#540→#542); deploy-workflow frontend flake filed (#539). **W7 externals closed (2026-07-02):** team onboarding discharged (six demo Entra users cross-shared at every ADR-0027 tier on the deployed app — no separate session, solo-dev) and KV purge-protection **decided: left off** (demo-scoped vault, destroy/re-apply flexibility; recorded in `deploy/README.md`) — and the last row closed 2026-07-03: **MCP client-config E2E passed** (#550 — a real VS Code `.vscode/mcp.json` against live `/mcp/` exercised all 8 tools end-to-end; client setup guide moved to `docs/mcp-setup.md`, README keeps a lean pointer + the trailing-slash `/mcp/` guidance) — **Week 7 is COMPLETE (41/41)**. Week-6 close: the **alerting backend track** — `ResultPublisher` seam (#366), Teams adaptive card + publisher (#367), severity-aware routing (#368), dedup (#369), suppression/snooze (#370), per-suite notification config (#373) — plus the **prototype Phase 5–6 screens**: Profile content (#374), Workspace Settings (#375), Admin layout-reconcile (#376), standard 4xx/5xx error pages (#377), and the per-suite **notification config UI** (#378). Earlier Week-6: Results scaffold (PR-C1), Enhanced Monitoring Dashboard + run-detail route (#333), results filter bar + orchestration poll/correlation (#347), drawer→page restructures (#350), layout/prototype-fidelity polish (#353), and redacted sample failing rows (#365, closed #226). **Week-7 early-credit:** Azure deploy scaffolding (#379 — frontend Dockerfile + ACA/SWA manifests + parameterized deploy workflow + CORS middleware + prod env reference; manual-trigger only, the actual apply stays blocked on Azure RP registration per ADR 0021). _All Week-6 feature work **merged to `main`** as the stacked PR chain #366→#379; follow-ups #380 (W6 close-out docs), #381 (deploy migration-gate + doc reconcile — a Week-7 CI/CD task landed early), and #390 (live run-progress per-status histogram fix, closes #316) merged after._ **Cloud-neutral cutover (2026-07-01, ADR 0028 §5 — DONE):** the deployed frontend moved **Static Web App → a Container App** (`dataq-app-frontend`), now the **sole public surface**, running the one generic nginx image with runtime `DATAQ_AUTH_*` OIDC config (**MSAL retired** for a generic oidc-client-ts; validated live as Olivia → dashboard). The **api moved to internal ingress** (reached only via the frontend nginx proxy `/api` + `/mcp` + `/healthz`); the SWA (`dataq-app-web`) is **destroyed**. Landed as **#509** (cutover) → **#510** (lifecycle guards — `ignore_changes` on container images so applies never roll prod back to `var.image_tag`, + on the api `identifier_uris` so applies never strip the token audience) → **#511** (three ACA gotchas: nginx must proxy **HTTP/1.1** or ACA ingress 426s; api ingress **HTTP + `allow_insecure_connections`**; **orphaned SWA-EasyAuth** on the api disabled via `az containerapp auth update --enabled false` — it 401'd every request post-SWA-destroy; DataQ does its own `fastapi-azure-auth` validation). Prod frontend image `:v2`; URL `https://dataq-app-frontend.purplefield-f7322a1b.westus2.azurecontainerapps.io`. Follow-up **#512** (multi-arch frontend QEMU build was impractically slow/flaky — 45-min stall → cancel) **fixed in #518**: pin the frontend Dockerfile `build` stage to `--platform=$BUILDPLATFORM` so the arch-neutral Vite build runs natively once and only the per-arch nginx `serve` stage differs — frontend publish dropped from a 45-min stall to **~1min**, and a native-arm64 `:latest` multi-arch is published reliably again. AWS/GCP deploy IaC stays post-v1 (#505).
287	**Week-8 exit gate:** ≥80% coverage gate enforced in CI across backend, API, frontend. — **met** (2026-07-03: backend/API gate `--cov-fail-under=80` on every pytest run incl. CI (#557); frontend all-src `lines: 80` via `pnpm test:coverage` in CI (#558); baselines 98.3% / 87.8% at flip).
```

> AGENT

Yes — I agree with prioritizing PATs, with one carve-out. Here's my thinking, plus a status note you need first.

**Status:** PR [#598](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/598) (the cycle-plan docs) is fully ready — review clean, all 14 CI checks green — but the permission system blocked me from squash-merging my own PR to protected `main`, so it's waiting for you to hit merge (or tell me explicitly to). All the GitHub state (milestones `v1.1 Week 1..6`, issues #587–#596, epic #597, the `DataQ Roadmap` project wiring) is already live.

**On PATs (#461):** you're right that it's the biggest unlocker in the backlog, and the subscription clock actually strengthens the case:

- It's the only thing that breaks the Azure-AD-only auth dependency **before** Azure winds down (W3). Once landed, every later live check — the W3 final E2E, `e2e_smoke.py`, the MCP NL spot-check #584 — runs on a DataQ bearer instead of the Azure-CLI token workaround (#565).
- It has to land **while Azure AD still works as the reference validator** — you mint a PAT from a logged-in session, so the Entra window is its natural deadline. Doing it early in the window beats doing it at the edge.
- It's the hard dependency for checks-as-code, headless MCP clients, and the whole ADR 0013 BYOL story — and the v1 retro itself said "schedule PATs early if programmatic/live use grows."
- Your "1 admin + 1 member" framing maps exactly onto the ADR 0026 shape: PATs are **user-scoped and inherit the owner's grants**, so you'd mint one as the workspace-admin user and one as a member (e.g. Olivia) — which conveniently re-creates the two-tier authz matrix from the go-live workout for all future headless testing. One caution: the admin PAT inherits workspace-admin powers, so give it a short expiry.

**The one thing that should still go first:** the Snowflake-live trio (#194/#195 live-verify + #587 baseline). That deadline is measured in *days*, not weeks — once the subscription lapses those become permanently un-verifiable, while PATs stay doable for the full 3-week Azure window. It's also only ~1–2 days of work before PATs get the rest of the week.

**Concrete reshuffle I'd make:** move #461 from W2 → **W1** (right behind the Snowflake trio, with #588's retirement of the leg able to run after), and push #532 (dry-run UC/flat-file — fully cloud-independent, no deadline) from W1 → W2 to balance. W2 then becomes OTel + secrets lifecycle + #584 running *on* the new PATs.

Since #598 is still unmerged, I can fold this into the same PR (progress.md + milestone + epic + project field updates) in one pass. Want me to?

```
{"results":[{"title":"Create services included with Azure free account","content":"# Create services included with Azure free account\nDuring the first 30 days after you've created an Azure free account, you have $200 credit in your billing currency to use on any service, except for third-party Marketplace purchases. You can experiment with different tiers and types of Azure services using the free credit to try out Azure. If you use services or Azure resources that aren’t free during that time, charges are deducted against your credit.\nIf you don’t use all of your credit by the end of the first 30 days, it's lost. After the first 30 days and up to 12 months after sign-up, you can only use a limited quantity of *some services*—not all Azure services are free. If you upgrade before 30 days and have remaining credit, you can use the rest of your credit with a pay-as-you-go subscription for the remaining days. For example, if you sign up for the free account on November 1 and upgrade on November 5, you have until November 30 to use your credit in the new pay-as-you-go subscription.\nYour Azure free account includes a *specified quantity* of free services for 12 months and a set of services that are always free. Only some tiers of services are available for free within certain quantities. For example, Azure has many virtual machines intended for different needs. The free account includes access to three types of VMs for free—the B1S, B2pts v2 (ARM-based), and B2ats v2 (AMD-based) burstable VMs that are usable for up to 750 hours per month. By staying in the free account limits, you can use the free services in various configurations. For more information about the Azure free account and the products that are available for free, see [Azure free account FAQ](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account?cid=msft_learn).","contentUrl":"https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/create-free-services"},{"title":"Avoid charges with your Azure free account","content":"# Avoid charges with your Azure free account\nEligible new users get $200 Azure credit in your billing currency for the first 30 days and a limited quantity of free services for 12 months with your [Azure free account](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account?cid=msft_learn). To learn about limits of free services, see the [Azure free account FAQ](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account?cid=msft_learn). As long as you have unexpired credit or you use only free services within the limits, you're not charged.\nLet's look at some of the reasons you can incur charges on your Azure free account.\n## Your credit runs out or is expired\nYour subscription and services are disabled when your credit runs out or expires at the end of 30 days. To continue using Azure services, you must upgrade your account. For more information, see [Upgrade your Azure free account](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/upgrade-azure-subscription). After you upgrade, you'll have continued access to free services for 12 months and you get charged only for usage beyond the free services and quantities.\n## Usage exceeds the limits of free services\nYou get a limited quantity of free services each month with your Azure free account. The free quantity expires at the end of the month and doesn't roll over to the next month. For example, you get 5 GB of File storage each month. If in a month, you only use 2 GB, the remaining 3 GB doesn't roll over to the next month. To avoid getting charged, keep your usage within the limits. To learn about limits of free services, see the [Azure free account FAQ](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account?cid=msft_learn). To check your free service usage, see [Check usage of free services included with Azure free Account](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/check-free-service-usage). If your usage exceeds the specified quantity of free services, you'll need to pay for the exceeding amount at pay-as-you-go rates. Microsoft will you send you an email notifying you when your credits are exhausted.","contentUrl":"https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/avoid-charges-free-account"},{"title":"Upgrade your Azure free account or Azure for Students Starter account","content":"# Upgrade your Azure free account or Azure for Students Starter account\n## Upgrade your Azure free account\nWhen you upgrade your Azure free account, you keep your remaining credit for the full 30 days from when you signed up. [![Screenshot that shows search.](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/media/upgrade-azure-subscription/search-subscriptions.png)](media/upgrade-azure-subscription/search-subscriptions.png#lightbox)\n3. Select the subscription that was created when you signed up for Azure free account.\n4. In the subscription overview, select **Upgrade subscription** in the command bar. You get $200 Azure credit in your billing currency and 12 months of free services on upgrade. If you don't qualify for a free account, you can upgrade to [pay-as-you-go rates](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0003p?cid=msft_learn) with a [support request](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?linkid=2083458).\nIf you have an [Azure for Students](https://azure.microsoft.com/offers/ms-azr-0170p/) account, you can upgrade to [pay-as-you-go rates](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/offers/ms-azr-0003p?cid=msft_learn).\nNote\nIf you use resources that aren’t free and your subscription gets disabled because you run out of credit, and then you upgrade your subscription, the resources get enabled after upgrade. This situation will result in you getting charged for the resources used.","contentUrl":"https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/upgrade-azure-subscription"},{"title":"Try Azure SQL Database free with Azure free account (old offer)","content":"# Try Azure SQL Database free with Azure free account (old offer)\n## Monitor and track service usage of previous free offer\n1. For more information, see [**Azure free account FAQ**](https://azure.microsoft.com/free/free-account-faq/).\n## Clean up resources\nIf you're using the Azure Database for PostgreSQL flexible server instance for development, testing, or predictable, time-bound production workloads, optimize usage by starting and stopping the server on-demand. After you stop the server, it remains in that state for seven days unless restarted sooner. For more information, see Start/Stop Server to lower TCO. When your Azure Database for PostgreSQL flexible server instance is stopped, there's no Compute usage, but Storage usage is still considered.\nAlternatively, if you don't expect to need these resources in the future, you can delete them by deleting the resource group, or you can just delete the Azure Database for PostgreSQL flexible server instance.\n1. You can check your service usage in the Azure portal. You can find out when your free services expire in the Azure portal.\n1. Sign in to the [Azure portal](https://portal.azure.com).\n2. In the left navigation area, select **All services**.\n3. If you don’t intend to use any Azure service, you can [cancel your subscription](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/cancel-azure-subscription).\n## Need help? Your Azure account is the credential you use to sign into Azure services like the [Azure portal](https://portal.azure.com) or [Cloud Shell](https://shell.azure.com).\n## Option 1: Use monthly Azure credits for Visual Studio subscribers\nIf you have a Visual Studio subscription, your subscription includes credits for using Azure. Activate your credits by visiting the [Monthly Azure credits for Visual Studio subscribers](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/member-offers/credit-for-visual-studio-subscribers/) page.\n## Option 2: Sign up for a free Azure account\nYou can create an [Azure account for free](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account?cid=msft_learn) and receive 12 months of popular services for free and a $200 credit to explore Azure for 30 days.\n## Option 3: Sign up for a pay-as-you-go account\nYou can also create a [pay-as-you-go Azure account](https://azure.microsoft.com/pricing/purchase-options/azure-account?cid=msft_learn). This option includes monthly amounts of select services for free, and charges you for what you use beyond the free limits. There is no upfront commitment and you can cancel anytime.\n## Option 4: Use a corporate account\nIf you are using Azure at work, talk to your company's cloud administrator to get your Azure credentials and then sign in to your account with the [Azure portal](https://portal.azure.com).","contentUrl":"https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/azure/create-azure-account"},{"title":"Try Azure SQL Managed Instance for free","content":"# Try Azure SQL Managed Instance for free\n## What's included in the free offer\n^1^ Since system files can take up to 32 GB of storage, the available storage for user data might be less than 64 GB. ^2^ Free instances that use the [Next-gen General Purpose](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/service-tiers-next-gen-general-purpose-use?view=azuresql) service tier upgrade are limited to 300 IOPS, and the IOPS slider is unavailable.\nAdditionally, the following limitations apply:\n1. The offer is valid for 12 months from the day you activate the offer. You can apply it to only one instance at a time, per subscription.\n2. After 12 months, the free SQL managed instance stops. If you don't upgrade the instance to a paid version within 30 days, the instance and all databases are deleted and no longer recoverable.\n3. The following capabilities aren't supported: [Zone redundancy](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/high-availability-sla-local-zone-redundancy?view=azuresql#zone-redundant-availability), [Failover groups](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/failover-group-sql-mi?view=azuresql), [Long-term backup retention](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/long-term-retention-overview?view=azuresql), modifying the backup storage redundancy.\n4. You can only scale up and down within the free offer limits.\n5. The free offer is available in all [regions that support Azure SQL Managed Instance](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/region-availability?view=azuresql).\n6. If you delete a free SQL managed instance, you delete all its databases and can't restore them.\n### Monthly vCore limits\nThe monthly free limits include 720 vCore hours of compute. Your free month of credits starts when you create your instance and renews on the same day of the following month.\nWhen you reach the monthly free vCore limit, the instance stops with a status of `Stopped - Insufficient credit`. A banner on the **Overview** page of your instance in the Azure portal gives you the option to create a new paid instance. You can [restore your database](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/point-in-time-restore?view=azuresql#restore-an-existing-database) to the new instance to continue your business without the limits imposed by the free offer.","contentUrl":"https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/managed-instance/free-offer?view=azuresql#what's-included-in-the-free-offer"},{"title":"Why is my Azure for Students subscription disabled and how do I reactivate it?","content":"# Why is my Azure for Students subscription disabled and how do I reactivate it?\nYour Azure for Students subscription might get disabled because you used all of your credit. It might also get disabled if your credit is expired or you accidentally canceled your subscription. See what issue applies to you and learn how you can get your subscription reactivated.\n## You used all of your credit\nAzure for Students account gives you $100 in credit and a limited quantity of free services for 12 months. Any usage beyond the free services and quantities is deducted from your credit. Once your credit runs out, Azure disables your services and subscription. To continue using Azure services, you must upgrade your subscription to a pay-as-you-go subscription by contacting [Azure support](https://portal.azure.com/?#blade/Microsoft_Azure_Support/HelpAndSupportBlade). After upgrade, your subscription still has access to free services for 12 months from your sign-up date. You only get charged for usage beyond the free services and quantities.\nYou can check your remaining credit on the [Microsoft Azure Sponsorships portal](https://www.microsoftazuresponsorships.com/balance).\n1. Sign in using your Azure for Students account credentials.\n2. The balance page gives information about used and remaining credit. You can find your credit expiration date below the credit chart.\n ![Screenshot that shows credit information for Azure for Students subscription.](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/media/azurestudents-subscription-disabled/azurestudents-credit-balance.png)\n3. To check usage of services that are consuming your Azure credit, select **Usage** from the left navigation then select your Azure for Students subscription.\n ![Screenshot that shows usage information for Azure for Students credit.](https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/media/azurestudents-subscription-disabled/azurestudents-credit-usage.png)\nThe table contains the following columns:\n1. **Service Name:** Service that is consuming your Azure credit.\n2. **Service Resource:** Unit of measurement for the service being consumed.\n3. **Spend:** Amount of credit in USD($) spent on the service.","contentUrl":"https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/cost-management-billing/manage/azurestudents-subscription-disabled"}]}
```
