> AGENT

Done. The script is at **`~/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/scripts/harness_window.sh`** (harness-side, deliberately not in the repo per ADR 0021). For ad-hoc testing you just call:

```bash
~/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/scripts/harness_window.sh window        # start → run flows → stop, one shot
~/Coding/Python/DataQ-harness/scripts/harness_window.sh window --adf  # same, + Flow-A ADF pipelines (needs live Snowflake)
```

Or the pieces individually: `status` (read-only), `start`, `run [--adf]`, `stop`.

What it does:

- **`start`** wakes things in dependency order — redis → Airflow scheduler (waits for `/health` to go green) → workers → trigger app — then starts both ADF triggers and resumes the 5 mockdata cron jobs. It reminds you it's billing (~CAD 0.70/hour while awake).
- **`run`** kicks all 5 mockdata job executions in parallel and polls each to completion (they land fresh files, which the ADF blob trigger picks up while awake). `--adf` additionally `create-run`s `pl_flow_a_customers` + `pl_flow_a_orders` and polls them — gated behind a flag because Flow A loads Snowflake, which dies this week (#588). Airflow DAGs aren't force-triggered; they fire on their own cron while the scheduler is awake (the script prints the UI URL if you want to trigger one manually).
- **`stop`** reverses everything: ADF triggers off, jobs suspended, apps stopped in reverse order, each verified `Stopped`.

Verification status, honestly: `bash -n` clean, and `status` ran against the live subscription — it correctly shows all four apps `Stopped`, both triggers `Stopped`, and the last (pre-suspension) job executions. The stop/suspend/trigger/executions ARM calls are the exact ones proven earlier today; the only paths not yet exercised are app `/start` and the Airflow health wait, since running them would wake the harness and spend credit. First real use — likely the W3 final live E2E, or your next ad-hoc test — is the true end-to-end validation; if `start` misbehaves I'd suspect only the health-wait timing, not the calls.

Also recorded: a pointer comment on [#590](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/590) (the script supersedes the manual runbook there) and the cross-session memory note now references the script path.

> DEVELOPER

the schedule jobs will run as manual with this script right?

> AGENT

Mostly yes, with one exception worth spelling out — and one edge I can verify right now for a fraction of a cent.

- **Mockdata jobs (the 5 ACA cron jobs): yes.** `run` fires them via the on-demand `start` endpoint — they execute immediately as manual executions, no waiting for their cron. The cron itself is also resumed during a window, so if a job's scheduled time happens to land inside your window it can *additionally* fire once on schedule.
- **ADF (`--adf`): yes.** `create-run` is a manual pipeline run, bypassing the daily schedule trigger (which, in a short window, would likely never fire on its own). Note the blob-event trigger is live during the window, so `pl_flow_a_orders` may also fire automatically when the mockdata files land — you can see it twice.
- **Airflow DAGs: no — the exception.** The script doesn't force-trigger DAG runs; they only fire if their cron lands inside the window (or you trigger manually in the Airflow UI). I can add a `--dags` flag that POSTs to the Airflow REST API (`/api/v1/dags/{id}/dagRuns`) if you want full one-shot coverage — it needs the Airflow credential from Key Vault.

DataQ-side: manual runs are ingested exactly like scheduled ones (the 10-min poll picks up all runs regardless of how they were triggered), so trigger bindings still fire on success.

One edge to verify: whether a manual `start` works while a job is **suspended** (matters if someone calls `run` without `start` first). Let me test that now — one mockdata job, costs ~a cent, no downstream fires since the blob trigger is stopped.

Use an IMMUTABLE tag, not 'latest' — Container Apps caches 'latest' at the node, so a same-tag rebuild won't be re-pulled on a new revision. Bump this on every DAG/image change. ### Pause Airflow DAG using cURL with Basic Authentication

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/clients/python/README.md

This cURL command demonstrates how to pause an Apache Airflow DAG via the API using a PATCH request. It includes setting the 'Content-Type' header to 'application/json', providing basic authentication credentials, and sending a JSON payload to update the 'is_paused' status of the specified DAG.

```bash
curl -X PATCH 'https://example.com/api/v1/dags/{dag_id}?update_mask=is_paused' \
-H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
--user "username:password" \
-d '{
    "is_paused": true
}'
```

--------------------------------

### Deprecated Airflow REST API Endpoints for DAGs, DAG Runs, and Tasks

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/docs/apache-airflow/deprecated-rest-api-ref.rst

This section documents the deprecated REST API endpoints related to DAGs, DAG runs, and tasks within Apache Airflow. It includes methods for triggering DAGs, retrieving DAG run information, and accessing task details. Note that this API is disabled by default and requires 'enable_experimental_api' to be set to 'True' in the '[api]' section of the Airflow configuration to be active.

```APIDOC
POST /api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/dag_runs
  - Creates a dag_run for a given DAG ID.
  - Note: If execution_date is not specified, Airflow creates only one DAG run per second for a given DAG_ID. To create multiple DagRuns within one second, set 'replace_microseconds' to 'false' (boolean as string).
  - Parameters:
    - DAG_ID: The ID of the DAG.
    - Request Body (JSON):
      - conf (optional): Configuration dictionary for the DAG run (e.g., {"key":"value"}).
      - execution_date (optional): The execution date for the DAG run in 'YYYY-mm-DDTHH:MM:SS.ssssss' format.
      - replace_microseconds (optional): Boolean as string ('true' or 'false') to allow multiple DAG runs within one second if 'false'.

GET /api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/dag_runs
  - Returns a list of Dag Runs for a specific DAG ID.
  - Parameters:
    - DAG_ID: The ID of the DAG.

GET /api/experimental/dags/<string:dag_id>/dag_runs/<string:execution_date>
  - Returns a JSON with a dag_run's public instance variables.
  - Parameters:
    - dag_id: The ID of the DAG.
    - execution_date: The execution date of the DAG run in 'YYYY-mm-DDTHH:MM:SS' format (e.g., '2016-11-16T11:34:15').

GET /api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/tasks/<TASK_ID>
  - Returns information for a specific task within a DAG.
  - Parameters:
    - DAG_ID: The ID of the DAG.
    - TASK_ID: The ID of the task.

GET /api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/dag_runs/<string:execution_date>/tasks/<TASK_ID>
  - Returns a JSON with a task instance's public instance variables for a specific DAG run.
  - Parameters:
    - DAG_ID: The ID of the DAG.
    - execution_date: The execution date of the DAG run in 'YYYY-mm-DDTHH:MM:SS' format (e.g., '2016-11-16T11:34:15').
    - TASK_ID: The ID of the task.

GET /api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/paused/<string:paused>
  - Sets the paused state of a DAG.
  - Parameters:
    - DAG_ID: The ID of the DAG.
    - paused: Must be 'true' to pause a DAG and 'false' to unpause.

GET /api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/paused
  - Returns the paused state of a DAG.
  - Parameters:
    - DAG_ID: The ID of the DAG.

GET /api/experimental/latest_runs
  - Returns the latest DagRun for each DAG, formatted for the UI.

GET /api/experimental/lineage/<DAG_ID>/<string:execution_date>/
  - Returns the lineage information for a specific DAG run.
  - Parameters:
    - DAG_ID: The ID of the DAG.
    - execution_date: The execution date of the DAG run.
```

```bash
curl -X POST \
    'http://localhost:8080/api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/dag_runs' \
    --header 'Cache-Control: no-cache' \
    --header 'Content-Type: application/json' \
    --data '{"conf":"{\"key\":\"value\"}"}'
```

```bash
curl -X POST  \
    'http://localhost:8080/api/experimental/dags/<DAG_ID>/dag_runs' \
    --header 'Content-Type: application/json' \
    --header 'Cache-Control: no-cache' \
    --data '{"replace_microseconds":"false"}'
```

### Airflow Stable API Permissions: DAG and DAG Run Management Endpoints

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/docs/apache-airflow-providers-fab/auth-manager/access-control.rst

Outlines the permissions required for various operations on DAGs and DAG Runs through the Airflow Stable API, covering reading DAGs, editing DAGs, clearing task instances, and managing DAG Runs.

```APIDOC
Endpoint: /dags
Method: GET
Permissions: DAGs.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Lists all DAGs.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}
Method: GET
Permissions: DAGs.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Retrieves details for a specific DAG by ID.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}
Method: PATCH
Permissions: DAGs.can_edit
Minimum Role: User
Description: Updates an existing DAG by ID.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/clearTaskInstances
Method: POST
Permissions: DAGs.can_edit, DAG Runs.can_read, Task Instances.can_edit
Minimum Role: User
Description: Clears task instances for a specific DAG.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/details
Method: GET
Permissions: DAGs.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Retrieves detailed information for a specific DAG.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/tasks
Method: GET
Permissions: DAGs.can_read, Task Instances.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Lists tasks for a specific DAG.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/tasks/{task_id}
Method: GET
Permissions: DAGs.can_read, Task Instances.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Retrieves details for a specific task within a DAG.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns
Method: GET
Permissions: DAGs.can_read, DAG Runs.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Lists DAG runs for a specific DAG.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns
Method: POST
Permissions: DAGs.can_edit, DAG Runs.can_create
Minimum Role: User
Description: Creates a new DAG run for a specific DAG.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id}
Method: DELETE
Permissions: DAGs.can_edit, DAG Runs.can_delete
Minimum Role: User
Description: Deletes a specific DAG run by ID.

Endpoint: /dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id}
Method: GET
Permissions: DAGs.can_read, DAG Runs.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Retrieves details for a specific DAG run by ID.
```

### Trigger Airflow DAG in JavaScript

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/airflow/www/templates/airflow/dags.html

This JavaScript function triggers an Apache Airflow DAG by sending a POST request to the specified link using the `postAsForm` utility. It's designed to initiate a DAG run programmatically from the frontend, typically when a user clicks a trigger button. The function prevents default link behavior by returning false.

```javascript
function triggerDag(link, dagId) {
  postAsForm(link.href, {});
  return false;
}
```

--------------------------------

### Trigger Airflow DAG from CLI

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/docs/apache-airflow/core-concepts/dag-run.rst

This snippet shows the basic command-line interface (CLI) command to manually trigger an Airflow DAG run. It allows specifying a logical date and an optional run ID for the triggered DAG, associating it with the trigger's timestamp.

```bash
airflow dags trigger --exec-date logical_date run_id
```

### Example Experimental API Endpoint for DAG Run Filtering

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/docs/apache-airflow/howto/upgrading-from-1-10/index.rst

This bash snippet illustrates the structure of an experimental Airflow API endpoint used for filtering DAG runs by execution date. This functionality is now handled by query parameters in the stable API v1.

```bash
/api/experimental/dags/<string:dag_id>/dag_runs/<string:execution_date>
```

--------------------------------

### API Endpoints for DAGRunApi

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/clients/python/README.md

Details the available API endpoints and their operations for managing DAG run resources within Apache Airflow.

```APIDOC
POST /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id}/clear - Clear a DAG run
DELETE /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id} - Delete a DAG run
GET /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id} - Get a DAG run
GET /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns - List DAG runs
POST /api/v1/dags/~/dagRuns/list - List DAG runs (batch)
GET /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id}/upstreamDatasetEvents - Get dataset events for a DAG run
POST /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns - Trigger a new DAG run
PATCH /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id}/setNote - Update the DagRun note.
PATCH /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns/{dag_run_id} - Modify a DAG run
```

### DAG Pause Status and Trigger/Cancel Buttons

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/airflow/www/templates/airflow/trigger.html

This Jinja2 fragment checks if the DAG is paused using `dag.get_is_paused()`. If it is, a message prompting to unpause is displayed. It then renders 'Trigger' and 'Cancel' buttons, with the cancel button linking to the `origin` URL.

```Jinja2
{% if dag.get_is_paused() %}

 Unpause DAG when triggered

{% endif %} Trigger [Cancel]({{ origin }})

{% endblock %} {% block tail_js %} {{ super() }} {% endblock %}
```

--------------------------------

### Airflow Stable API Permissions: DAG Source Code Endpoint

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/docs/apache-airflow-providers-fab/auth-manager/access-control.rst

Specifies the permissions necessary to access the source code of a DAG via the Airflow Stable API.

```APIDOC
Endpoint: /dagSources/{file_token}
Method: GET
Permissions: DAG Code.can_read
Minimum Role: Viewer
Description: Retrieves the source code for a DAG using its file token.
```

### Configure Airflow to Load Example DAGs

Source: https://github.com/apache/airflow/blob/2.10.5/clients/python/README.md

This configuration enables the loading of example DAGs in Airflow, which is crucial for running the provided smoke tests. It can be set in the `airflow.cfg` file under the `[core]` section or via the `AIRFLOW__CORE__LOAD_EXAMPLES` environment variable.

```INI
[core]
load_examples = True
```

  "new_string": "cmd_dags() {\n  local user pass d rid state\n  user=$(terraform -chdir=\"$TF_DIR\" output -raw airflow_admin_user)\n  pass=$(terraform -chdir=\"$TF_DIR\" output -raw airflow_admin_password)\n  log \"Triggering the cron DAGs via the Airflow REST API (sequential)...\"\n  for d in \"${DAGS[@]}\"; do\n    # a dagRun created on a paused DAG sits queued forever -> unpause first (idempotent)\n    curl -sf --max-time 30 -X PATCH \"$AIRFLOW_URL/api/v1/dags/$d?update_mask=is_paused\" \\\n      -H 'Content-Type: application/json' --user \"$user:$pass\" -d '{\"is_paused\": false}' >/dev/null\n    rid=$(curl -sf --max-time 30 -X POST \"$AIRFLOW_URL/api/v1/dags/$d/dagRuns\" \\\n      -H 'Content-Type: application/json' --user \"$user:$pass\" -d '{}' |\n      python3 -c \"import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['dag_run_id'])\")\n    log \"  $d -> $rid\"\n    for _ in $(seq 1 60); do\n      state=$(curl -sf --max-time 30 --user \"$user:$pass\" \\\n        \"$AIRFLOW_URL/api/v1/dags/$d/dagRuns/$rid\" |\n        python3 -c \"import json,sys; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['state'])\" 2>/dev/null || echo unknown)\n      [[ \"$state\" != \"queued\" && \"$state\" != \"running\" && \"$state\" != \"unknown\" ]] && break\n      sleep 15\n    done\n    log \"  $d: $state\"\n  done\n}\n\ncmd_run() {\n  local with_adf=\"\" with_dags=\"\" a j execs=()\n  for a in \"$@\"; do\n    [[ \"$a\" == \"--adf\" ]] && with_adf=1\n    [[ \"$a\" == \"--dags\" ]] && with_dags=1\n  done"
}

!/usr/bin/env bash
harness_window.sh — open/close an ad-hoc DataQ harness test window on Azure.

The harness compute was stopped 2026-07-04 (cost wind-down, DataQ #590): while
awake it burns ~CAD 17/day (~0.70/hour); stopped it costs ~0. This script wakes
it, optionally kicks the data flows, and puts it back to sleep.

> AGENT

Done — here's what the Airflow docs check found and what changed in the script.

**Docs findings (Airflow 2.10.5, the harness's exact version):**
- The stable REST API is the right surface: **`POST /api/v1/dags/{dag_id}/dagRuns`** with an empty JSON body triggers a manual run; **basic auth** works because the harness sets `REDACTED,session` (the TF comment says it's kept exactly for DataQ's poller). The admin user clears the required role (POST dagRuns needs ≥User).
- One documented gotcha worth the check: **a run created on a paused DAG never executes** — it just queues. So the script PATCHes `is_paused: false` (with `update_mask`) before triggering, idempotently.
- Run status polls `GET /api/v1/dags/{id}/dagRuns/{run_id}` until it leaves `queued`/`running`.

**Script updates** (`harness_window.sh`, syntax-checked):
- New **`--dags` flag** on `run`/`window`: unpauses + manually triggers the three cron DAGs sequentially (`flow_a_snowflake_load` → `flow_a_uc_reference` → `flow_b_medallion`, mirroring their cron ordering) and reports each terminal state. Credentials come from the harness Terraform outputs (`airflow_admin_user`/`_password` — verified they resolve locally, no Key Vault dependency).
- `flow_a_payments_event` is deliberately **not** force-triggered: it's `schedule=None` and expects an Event-Grid `conf['blob_url']` payload — it fires naturally when the mockdata jobs land files while the harness is awake. And `flow_a_snowflake_load` will start failing once Snowflake lapses (#588), same caveat as `--adf`.
- Flags now combine: `harness_window.sh window --adf --dags` is the full one-shot.

Also, closing the loop on your previous question with test evidence: a suspended job **refuses** manual starts (verified — the API errors with "Job is suspended"), so I fixed `run` to resume → start → re-suspend, and proved the sequence live: execution `mockdata-feedback-d7wvjl5` ran to **Succeeded** and the job is back to suspended (with a 409-retry added, since suspend races the start's provisioning). The only path not yet exercised end-to-end is the DAG trigger itself, since that requires waking Airflow (~CAD 0.70/hour) — it'll get its first real validation on your next test window or the W3 final E2E.

> AGENT

The full window test **passed end-to-end, 11.5 minutes, exit 0** — every leg of the script is now verified on real infra:

| Phase | Result |
|---|---|
| Wake (redis → Airflow `/health` → workers → trigger → ADF triggers) | up in 2.7 min |
| 5 mockdata jobs (manual executions) | all `Succeeded`, re-suspended after |
| 3 cron DAGs via REST (`snowflake_load`, `uc_reference`, `flow_b_medallion`) | all `success` |
| 2 ADF pipelines (`pl_flow_a_customers`, `pl_flow_a_orders`) | both `Succeeded` |
| Sleep (triggers → jobs → apps, reverse order) | everything back to `Stopped`, each verified |

Cost of the test: ~11 minutes awake ≈ **CAD 0.15**. Burn is back to the ~CAD 4.5/day app-only baseline.

Two bonuses from the run: Snowflake is confirmed **still alive** — that was an unplanned final green run of Flows A and B before the lapse (the W1 items #194/#195/#587 still deserve their day-1 slot while this holds). And DataQ's 10-minute poller will have ingested these ADF/Airflow runs, so the Results → pipeline-runs tab should show fresh entries from 19:42–19:50 UTC if you want an eyeball check in the app.

The script is fully production-ready for your ad-hoc windows: `harness_window.sh window --adf --dags` for everything, or `start` / `run` / `stop` piecemeal. The wake runbook comment on [#590](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/590) already points to it.

Note: assigning to an already-closed milestone can't be done via `gh issue edit --milestone <title>` — use the REST API with the milestone *number* (`gh api -X PATCH repos/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/issues/<N> -F milestone=<num>`).
93	- **Backlink is the source of truth.** Always include the /review comment URL or source PR number so the issue can be cross-referenced.

 ## How this maps to GitHub
 
-- **Status** lives on the GitHub `Backlog (post-v1 / testing)` milestone — this doc mirrors it by theme.
+- **Status** lives on GitHub — un-scheduled issues sit on the `v1.1 Backlog` milestone; scheduled ones move to a `v1.1 Week N` milestone (see `docs/progress.md` §Cycle plan). This doc mirrors the backlog by theme.
 - When you pick up a theme, **file the design-only items** (Themes 2, 3, 4, 5, 8, 9, 11, 14 carry them) as issues on that milestone first.
-- New post-v1 work: open the issue, milestone it `Backlog (post-v1 / testing)`, and add a row to the
+- New post-v1 work: open the issue, milestone it `v1.1 Backlog` (or the target week if already scheduled), and add a row to the
   matching theme here. Keep the detailed *design* in the three linked docs, not in this index.

> AGENT

CI is running on PR [#600](https://github.com/TheurgicDuke771/DataQ/pull/600) — I'll merge when green. Status of the remap so far, all live on GitHub:

- **Milestone renamed** to `v1.1 Backlog` (description updated to explain the flow: issues graduate from it to a `v1.1 Week N` milestone when scheduled).
- **8 week-fit issues moved and fully wired** (milestone + project `v1.1 week` field + epic #597 checklists): #520 → W4 (flat-file freshness/volume, same engine code as the schema_drift work) · #424, #349 → W5 (results-surface week) · #278, #322, #571, #541, #306 → W6 (test-hardening + small-bug batches). **31 issues stay backlogged** — compliance, alerting nits, the frontend-refactor batch, and the big next-cycle features, per the roadmap themes.
- **Planning correction found en route:** #492 (ADF webhook delivery) had been scheduled into W3, but it actually **closed 2026-07-02** — the metric-alert delivery path shipped and was verified live during the W7 smoke. Re-homed to its Week-7 milestone; W3 is now 3 rows.
- **Final week loads:** W1 6 · W2 5 · W3 3 · W4 5 · W5 5 · W6 12 — 66 open issues total (34 scheduled + epic + 31 backlog), verified against GitHub.
- **Docs PR #600** updates progress.md plus every live reference to the old milestone name — including the `gh-issue-from-finding` skill, which files issues by milestone *title* and would have silently broken on the rename. The frozen `progress-v1.md` keeps the historical name. Review caught two count inconsistencies (W4 subtotal, project-wiring count), fixed.
